### T037e31d2664cbcdb321a7e3e

Text hash 2740e0e059480ec9aeead16245f20fbc7db757ff5d95f01e72f09b193024a711; absolute characters 0–529; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6027-exemption-john-storey-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
By virtue of the authority vested in me by section 204 of Part II of the act entitled "AN ACT Making appropriations for the Legislative Branch of the Government for the fiscal year ending June 30, 1933, and for other purposes," approved June 30, 1932, John Storey, gardener, Public Buildings and Public Parks of the National Capital, who, during the current month, has reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, is hereby exempted from the provisions of that section until February 28, 1935.
```


### T05ed73311c1b138800380ce4

Text hash e24db7cd369c7fd8b0781676efb2e31abc64dae0a35207f5d5763704ac2a8c29; absolute characters 0–1279; files: jobs/mabillon-held-printings/output/texts/EO_6205C.txt

```text
mee

vita EE! y ‘

—6Executibe Order

[Sik AssocIATION oF AMERICA, CoDE oF Farr COMPETITION]

Pursuant to the authority vested in me by Title I of the National Industrial
Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and pending action upon a Code of Fair
Competition to be presented by the Silk Association of America,

I agree with the Committee representing the Broad Silk and Rayon Weavers
Division, the Converters Division, the Special Fabrics Division, the Ribbon Division,
and the Woven Label Division, of the Silk Association of America, that they shall be
bound beginning July 17 by the provisions of the Cotton Textile Industry Code as
set forth in the telegram, dated July 14, offering this agreement to the President of
the United States, pursuant to Section 4 of the National Recovery Act, which tele-
gram is signed by Henry E. Stehli, James C. Black, Paul C. Debry, Sol C. Moss,
Ramsay Peugnet, George G. Sommaripa, and addressed to Mr. Nelson Slater, Deputy
Administrator, Department of Commerce, Washington, D.C., with the express
understanding that this agreement is subject to cancellation at any time without

notice.
FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT.

Approval recommended:
Huau S. Jounson.

Tue Waite Hovss,
July 15, 1933.

[No. 6205-C]

U.S. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1933

```

```text
DEPOSITED BY THE
UNITED STATES OF AMERICA

MAR 30 '33

6!
Aé

Executive @rder

TRANSFER OF CONTROL AND JURISDICTION OF CERTAIN BUILDINGS AT
CRISTOBAL TO THE Navy DEPARTMENT

CANAL ZONE

By virtue of the authority vested in me by law, it is hereby ordered that Buildings
Nos. 1113, 1409, and 1411, located at Cristobal, Canal Zone, that were transferred,
exclusive of the sites thereof but including free use of such sites, by Executive Order
No. 3917, dated October 16, 1923, from the Panama Canal to the War Department, be,
and the same are hereby, transferred under like conditions to the Navy Department.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
Tue Wuite House,
March 8, 19383.

[No. 6072]

U.S. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1089

```

### T1b6fe9f24e31bb816709b194

Text hash 297f2f4f0800226e631c67f1fb3523e027f0502201a24eedd738c1892d4a8593; absolute characters 0–1591; files: jobs/mabillon-held-printings/output/texts/EO_7208.txt

```text
€xecutive Groder

EXEMPTION OF GEORGE G. HEp@cocK FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT
FOR AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404
(5 U. S. C., sec. 692b), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch
or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the
District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for auto-
matic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in
such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary:
Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions

of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires:
’

es *£ 8 9,
?

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that George G. Hedgcock, senior
pathologist, Bureau of Plant Industry, Department of Agriculture, who was ex-
empted from compulsory retirement for a period of two years by Executive Orders
No. 6338 of October 14, 1933, and No. 6862 of September 30, 1934, be further
exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service until
November 1, 1936:

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in
me by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt George G. Hedgecock from the pro-
visions thereof and continue him in the service until November 1, 1936.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
Tue Waite Hovse,
October 19, 19386.

[No. 7208]

U.S. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1938

```

```text
EXEMPTION OF JOHN STOREY FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT FOR AGE
By virtue of the authority vested in me by section 204 of Part II of the act
entitled " AN ACT Making appropriations for the Legislative Branch of the Govern￾ment for the fiscal year ending June 30 , 1933 , and for other purposes ," approved
June 30, 1932 , John Storey , gardener , Public Buildings and Public Parks of the

prescribed for automatic separation from the service , is hereby exempted from the
provisions of that section until February 28 , 1935 .
```

### T355f91a370a862c3ac2df5cb

Text hash 7de535aa188e72c880a0680ab07021fd25c3d125b0ff2b40c6f68a384ec9180d; absolute characters 0–1383; files: jobs/mabillon-held-printings/output/texts/EO_6255.txt

```text

bac)
ON

Executive @rder

CopE oF Farr COMPETITION FOR THE LUMBER AND TIMBER
Propucts INDUSTRIES

An application having been duly made, pursuant to and in full compliance
with the provisions of title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved
June 16, 1933, for my approval of a Code of Fair Competition for the Lumber and
Timber Products Industries, and hearings having been held thereon and the Admin-
istrator having rendered his report containing an analysis of the said Code of Fair
Competition together with his recommendations and findings with respect thereto,
and the Administrator having found that the said Code of Fair Competition com-
plies in all respects with the pertinent provisions of title I of said act and that the
requirements of clauses (1) and (2) of subsection (a) of section 3 of the said act
have been met:

Now, therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States,
pursuant to the authority vested in me by title I of the National Industrial Recovery
Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do adopt and approve the report, recom-
mendations and findings of the Administrator and do order that the said Code of
Fair Competition be and it is hereby approved.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT.
Approval recommended:

Hues 8S. JoHNnson,
Administrator.

Tue Waite Hovss,
August 19, 1988.

[No. 6255]

U.S, GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1933

```

```text
Upon application by parties affected, and it appearing to me that justice requires a postponement of the effective date of article IX, section 4 of said code, in order to allow manufacturers of coupons and other forms of scrip the opportunity to adjust their business and in order to avoid a reduction of employment among such manufacturers, and pursuant to the authority vested in me by title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, it is hereby ordered that the effective date of article IX, section 4, be extended to July 1, 1934, this order to become effective within 10 days of the date hereof unless cause to the contrary shall have been shown to the Administrator before that date.
```

```text
2

provision A, which is substantially identical with the provision inadvertently
omitted as aforesaid:

“'The sale or offering for sale of any distillate burner of sleeve type that does
not meet the following specifications is unfair competition; provided, however, that
where a manufacturer of such equipment desires to manufacture a burner of this
type, with other materials than those specified, or desires to use a different combina-
tion of parts than those specified, such manufacturer shall first apply to the code
authority for permission to do so and he shall at the same time submit proof that the
substitutions requested will offer to the consumer protection and service equivalent
to that offered by a burner constructed as herein provided. The code authority will
pass upon this petition. If denied, the applicant may apply to the Administrator
for permission and the decision of the Administrator shall be final.

“(a) The burner top rings, top caps and sleeves shall have a thickness of not
less than 0.026’ and chromium content of not less than 16-18 percent, or alloys of
equivalent heat resistance.

“(b) The burner sleeves shall have a height of 434’’ or more.

“(c) Other construction and material specifications must comply with the
Underwriters’ Laboratories’ requirements as specified in their code of April, 1932
and amendments to June 1, 1933.

“(d) Distillate conversion burners (class 4(a)) must include all of the follow-
ing component parts:

il container and stand, hand control metering valve or valves; mininium of
6 feet of copper tubing with single burners, and 7 feet with multiple burners; suitable
fittings ; bases and supports, approved wicking, sleeves; top rings; caps and the
necessary bolts and screws for proper installation.

“(e) Quality of material, workmanship and packing must comply with the
Underwriters’ Laboratories’ requirements as specified in their code of April, 1932
and amendments to June 1, 1933.”

Pursuant to the authority vested in me by title I of the National Industrial
Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1938, upon due consideration of the facts herein-
above set forth, and upon the report and recommendation of the Administrator,

I, Frankliii D. Roosevelt, President of the United States, do hereby order that
the application of the code authority of the Oil Burner Industry hereinaWove cbh-
sidered, be approved, and that, effective 10 days from the date hereof, unléss just
cause to the contrary should be shown by any interested person before Deputy
Administrator R. B. Paddock, New Commerce Building, Washington, D.C., at or
before 12 o’clock, noon, October 13, 1933, the Code of Fair Competition for the Oil
Burner Industry as approved September 18, 1933 be and it hereby is amended by

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT.
```

```text
34

‘AG Executive Order

Cope oF Farr CoMPETITION FOR THE RETAIL TRADE
ORDER No. 7
EXTENSION OF EFFECTIVE DATE OF ARTICLE IX, SECTION 4

A Code of Fair Competition for the Retail Trade was approved by me October
21, 1933. Upon application by parties affected, and it appearing to me that justice
requires a postponement of the effective date of article IX, section 4 of said code, in
order to allow manufacturers of coupons and other forms of scrip the opportunity
to adjust their business and in order to avoid a reduction of employment among such
manufacturers, and pursuant to the authority vested in me by title I of the National
Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, it is hereby ordered
that the effective date of article IX, section 4, be extended to July 1, 1934, this order
to become effective within 10 days of the date hereof unless cause to the contrary
shall have been shown to the Administrator before that date.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT.
Approval recommended:
Hueu S. Jounson,
Administrator for Industrial Recovery.

Tae Warts Hovsz,
November 27, 1983.

[No. 6467]

```

```text
Executive Order 6061. March 2, 1933
Executive Order
APPOINTMENT OF SPECIAL INVESTIGATORS IN THE IMMIGRATION SERVICE ,
DEPARTMENT OF LABOR
The following -named persons may be appointed special investigators in the
Immigration Service , Department of Labor , without regard to the requirements of
the civil -service rules :
Joseph Gambaro Solomon Israel
This order is issued upon the recommendation of the Secretary of Labor , who

THE WHITE HOUSE ,
March 2, 1933 .
```

```text
FOR AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (5 U.S.C.,
sec. 715a), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch
or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the
District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for auto-
matic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in

Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions

of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires
* * md),
?

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that George G. Hedgcock, senior
pathologist, Bureau of Plant Industry, Department of Agriculture, who was exempted
from compulsory retirement for a period of three years by Executive Orders No. 6338
of October 14, 1933, No. 6862 of September 30, 1934, and No. 7208 of October 19,
1935, be further exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the
service until July 1, 1937;
```

```text
DEPOSITED BY THE
UNITED STATES OF AM


B |
AG

Executibe @rder

EXEMPTION OF CurTIS F. MARBUT FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT
For AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404;
5 U.S.C., sec. 692b), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch
or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the Dis-
trict of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic
separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such
service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided,
That the President may, by Executive order, exempt from the provisions of this
section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * *.”

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that Curtis F. Marbut, Chief of
the Division of Soil Survey of the Bureau of Chemistry and Soils, Department of
Agriculture, who was 70 years of age on July 19, 1933, and who was exempted until
June 30, 1934, from compulsory retirement by Executive Order No. 6214, dated
July 25, 1933, be further exempted from the provisions of this section and continued
in the service until July 1, 1935;

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the authority vested in me by the aforesaid
section, I do hereby exempt Curtis F. Marbut from the provisions thereof and con-
tinue him in the service until July 1, 1935.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT.
Tue Wairr Hovss,
June 14, 1984.

[No. 6738]

```

```text
The following appointments made by the President to classified positions in the Department of Labor without regard to the requirements of the civil-service rules, are upon the recommendation of the Secretary of Labor, hereby revoked.

(1) The appointment on February 16, 1933, of the following-named persons as Special Investigators in the Immigration Service, Department of Labor:

Wallace D. Bassford
J. Theodore George

Ervin F. Brown
James Hoover

W. Woodruff Chisum
Thomas A. Lewis

Carlo deVio
James H. O'Connor

Abraham Dickstein
Mabel Rewman

Edward S. Doak
Harry L. Tetlow

William L. Ford
John W. Williams

(2) The appointment on March 2, 1933, of the following-named persons as Special Investigators in the Immigration Service, Department of Labor:

Joseph Gambaro
Solomon Israel

(3) The appointment on March 2, 1933, of the following-named persons to classified positions in the Department of Labor:

Miss Ellen T. Purcell
Miss J. Elizabeth Williams

(4) The appointment on March 3, 1933, of Thomas H. Allen as Special Investigator in the Immigration Service, Department of Labor.

(5) The appointment on August 1, 1932, of Lamar Costello as Supervisor of Detentions in the Immigration Service, Department of Labor.

(6) The appointment on May 13, 1931, of Mrs. Dorothy I. Sinnott as Junior Clerk in the Naturalization Service, Department of Labor.

(7) The appointment on March 2, 1929, of Jeanette J. Donahue as Clerk in the Immigration Service, Department of Labor.

(8) The appointment on February 26, 1929, of Mrs. Julia Behake to a classified position in the Department of Labor.

(9) The appointment on October 23, 1926, of Mrs. Susan T. Waite as Clerk in the Bureau of Labor Statistics, Department of Labor.

(10) The appointment on October 21, 1929, of Estelle Roberts as Matron in the Immigration Service, Department of Labor.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

June 16, 1933.

```

```text
from the service, is hereby exempted from the provisions of that section until June
```

### T9d42f4ea41e7ea8edaf4c457

```text
Upon application by parties affected, and it appearing to me that justice requires a postponement of the effective date of article IX, section 4 of said code, in order to allow manufacturers of coupons and other forms of scrip the opportunity to adjust their business and in order to avoid a reduction of employment among such manufacturers, and pursuant to the authority vested in me by title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, it is hereby ordered that the effective date of article IX, section 4, be extended to July 1, 1934, this order to become effective within 10 days of the date hereof unless cause to the contrary shall have been shown to the Administrator before that date.
```

### Ta8aef756d564b760fc258eb1

Text hash 6a00b5647051d687781d887a4eeebea48b7a179712f937e8328cb5b355159d69; absolute characters 0–1566; files: jobs/mabillon-held-printings/output/texts/EO_6338.txt

```text
B |

Executive Order

EXEMPTION OF JAMES S. GOLDSMITH AND GEorGE G. HepGcock
FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT FOR AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404;
5 US.C., sec. 692b), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or
service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District
of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic
separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such
service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided,
That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this
section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires:”

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that James S. Goldsmith, Super-
intendent of Buildings and Labor of the United States National Museum, and George
G. Hedgecock, senior pathologist, Department of Agriculture, who, during the current
month, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the
service, be exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service
until October 31, 1934;

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the authority vested in me by the aforesaid
statute, I do hereby exempt James S. Goldsmith and George G. Hedgecock from the
provisions thereof and continue them in the service until October 31, 1934.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT.
THe Waite Hovss,
October 14, 1933.

[No. 6338]

U.S. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1933

```

```text
TION FOR THE UNDERWEAR AND ALLIED Propucts MANUFACTURING
INDUSTRY

A Code of Fair Competition for the Underwear and Allied Products Manufac-
turing Industry having been approved by me September 18, 1933, on condition that
all manufacturers included within the provisions of part I, section 1(c) of said code
be granted a stay of fourteen (14) days after the effective date thereof, during which
period they might show cause to the Administrator why they should not be included
under the provisions of such section, and public hearings having been held thereon
October 14, 1933,

NOW, THEREFORE, T, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States,
pursuant to the authority vested in me by title I of the National Industrial Recovery
Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do hereby grant an additional stay of
said provisions pending the determination by the Administrator of the issues with
respect thereto.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT.

Approval recommended:
Huan S. Jounson,
Administrator.

Tue Wuite Hovssz,
October 20, 1988.

[No. 6347]
```

```text
Executive Order 6004. January 19 , 1933
Executive Order
EXEMPTION OF OLIVER M. MAXAM FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT
FOR AGE
By virtue of the authority vested in me by section 204 of Part II of the act
entitled "AN ACT Making appropriations for the Legislative Branch of the Gov-
ernment for the fiscal year ending June 30, 1933 , and for other purposes ," approved
June 30 , 1932 , Oliver M. Maxam, Chief of the Division of Operations , United States
Coast Guard , Treasury Department , who , during the current month , reaches the
retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service , is hereby
exempted from the provisions of that section .
THE WHITE HOUSE ,
January 19 , 1933 .
HERBERT HOOVER
[No. 6004]

```

### Tb4acb7a18147386c53c9af59

Text hash 7be170819eddadb71d20f7642fd45703fd4fdb7967553709c694f755585ada35; absolute characters 0–3345; files: jobs/mabillon-held-printings/output/texts/EO_6998.txt

```text
DERI ier es

MNUTED race oe

2

5. No license or written authority issued under these regulations shall be trans-
ferred, nor shall the privileges granted thereby be exercised through an assignee,
contractor, or concessionaire.

6. The official by whose authority any license or written authority is issued
under these regulations may suspend or revoke such license or written authority for
a violation of any of the laws and regulations relating to the sale, manufacture, or
importation of alcoholic beverages, or for a violation of any of the conditions of the
license or written authority imposed under section 4 of these regulations.

7. All licenses issued under these regulations shall be kept posted under glass
in a conspicuous place on the premises where the alcoholic beverages are sold.

8. The right of the United States to permit the importation of alcoholic beverages
into the Canal Zone shall not be exercised while these regulations remain in effect:
Provided, That nothing herein shall be construed to restrict importations into the
Canal Zone of alcoholic beverages from the Republic of Panama, or the entry of
alcoholic beverages arriving at Canal Zone ports for ultimate delivery to points
beyond the Canal Zone.

9. The manufacture of alcoholic beverages in the Canal Zone is forbidden.

10. Nothing in these regulations shall apply to alcoholic liquors for sacramental,
scientific, pharmaceutical, industrial, or medicinal purposes.

11. Any person violating any of the provisions of these regulations shall be
punished by a fine of not more than $500 or imprisoned in jail for not more than 6
months, or by both, and in addition the license of any such person may be revoked
or suspended, as provided in the Act of June 19, 1934 (ch. 657, 48 Stat. 1116).

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT
Tue Wuite Hovss,
March 26, 1935.

[No. 6997]

U.S. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1938
```

### Tc9239e6a3dd092ba64084724

Text hash 7d55aa15b530d28560942df5226d2b23ed57cb4688f23d67937215bb746aad30; absolute characters 0–1324; files: jobs/mabillon-held-printings/output/texts/EO_6254.txt

```text
DEFGEIT TT HY THE

cal

Executive @rder

CopE oF Farr COMPETITION FOR THE IRON AND STEEL INDUSTRY

An application having been duly made, pursuant to and in full compliance with
the provisions of title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16,
1933, for my approval of a Code of Fair Competition for the Iron and Steel Industry,
and hearings having been held thereon and the Administrator having rendered his
report together with his recommendations and findings with respect thereto, and the
Administrator having found that the said Code of Fair Competition complies in all
respects with the pertinent provisions of title I of said act and that the requirements
of clauses (1) and (2) of subsection (a) of section 3 of the said act have been met:

Now, therefore, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States,
pursuant to the authority vested in me by title I of the National Industrial Recovery
Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do adopt and approve the report, recom-
mendations and findings of the Administrator and do order that the said Code of
Fair Competition be and it is hereby approved.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT.
Approval recommended:

Hvuau 8S. Jounson,
Administrator.

Tse Waite Hovsz,
August 19, 1933.

(No. 6254]

(1S. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1933
```

```text
Executive Order
```

```text
An application having been duly made, pursuant to and in full compliance with
the provisions of title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16,
1933, for my approval of a Code of Fair Competition for the American Petroleum
Equipment Industry and Trade, and hearings having been held thereon and the
Administrator having rendered his report containing an analysis of the said code of
fair competition together with his recommendations and findings with respect thereto,
and the Administrator having found that the said code of fair competition complies
in all respects with the pertinent provisions of title I of said act and that the require-
ments of clauses (1) and (2) of subsection (a) of section 3 of the said act have been
met:

NOW, THEREFORE, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States,
pursuant to the authority vested in me by title I of the National Industrial Recovery
Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do adopt and approve the report
and recommendations and findings of the Administrator and do order that the said
code of fair competition be and it is hereby approved.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT.

Hucu S. Jounson,

Administrator.

Tue Wuite House,
November 2, 1933.
```

### Td671517b73e6ff65fc6ed1ac

Text hash dead66e967d4d30621c31245e211455671510ae8b44864c2399b54a2e58a362d; absolute characters 0–1445; files: jobs/mabillon-held-printings/output/texts/EO_6457.txt

```text
DEPCSITES Ty THE
UNITES cic lb ee iCA

DEC 29 133

Executive Order

Cope or Farr CoMPETITION

CHINAWARE AND PORCELAIN MANUFACTURING INDUSTRY

An application having been duly made, pursuant to and in full compliance with
the provisions of title I of the National Industrial Recovery Act, approved June 16,
1933, for my approval of a Code of Fair Competition for the Chinaware and Porcelain
Manufacturing Industry, and hearings having been held thereon and the Adminis-
trator having rendered his report containing an analysis of the said code of fair com-
petition together with his recommendations and findings with respect thereto, and
the Administrator having found that the said code of fair competition complies in all
respects with the pertinent provisions of title I of said act and that the requirements
of clauses (1) and (2) of subsection (a) of section 3 of the said act have been met:

NOW, THEREFORE, I, Franklin D. Roosevelt, President of the United States,
pursuant to the authority vested in me by title I of the National Industrial Recovery
Act, approved June 16, 1933, and otherwise, do adopt and approve the report, recom-
mendations, and findings of the Administrator and do order that the said code of fair

competition be and is hereby approved.
FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT,
President.

Approval recommended:
Huazu S. Jonnson,

Administrator.

Tue Waite Hovse,
November 27, 1933.

(No. 6457]

U.S, GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE 195%

```

```text
# EO:6022A: Executive Order 6022 -A . February 10 , 1933
# Source: Proclamations and Executive Orders: Herbert Hoover, March 4, 1929 to March 4, 1933, Volume II (Executive Orders 5550-6070; Tables 1-3; Index) (Washington: U.S. Government Printing Office, 1974 (GPO stock no. 2203-00906)); Google, University of California copy (received Jan 6, 1975); HathiTrust handle 2027/uc1.c025504761
# PDF: releases/source_books/OFR_1974_Hoover_Proclamations_and_Executive_Orders_v2_uc1.c025504761.pdf sha256 3bce4d490526a69516217faf43b66b62adf565a388b622f6d1e9225faffdcdf5
# PDF pages 654-654; printed pages 1425-1425; closing tag match; confidence high
# Text: OCR text layer of the scan (pypdf 6.20.1 extract_text(), NUL characters removed; the layer is Google's OCR), running heads and page numbers removed. Not human-verified. Body sha256 8bd368856fcf1d063ec8f307013c3895472f0b2fc701e1ffff90a76205561162

Executive Order 6022 -A . February 10 , 1933
Executive Order
CLASSIFICATION OF MR. HARRY A. MCBRIDE
Harry A. McBride , who has been serving as an Assistant to the Secretary of
State without a competitive classified status since January 1, 1932 , is hereby classified .
Mr. McBride entered the service of the Department of State as a clerk in the
American Consulate at Dresden October 1, 1908, and served in a similar capacity
at Bilbao 1909, Barcelona 1910 , and Zürich 1911. In 1912 he was appointed , after
examination , a consular assistant in the career Consular Service and in 1917 was
promoted to the grade of consul . He served as an alternate American representative ,
Nitrate Executive, 1918, and as Acting General Receiver of Customs and Financial
Adviser of Liberia and member of the Liberian International Debt Commission ,
1918-19 . He resumed his duties in the Consular Service as Consul at Warsaw in
1919 and served on special detail in the Department of State , 1920-1922 . In 1923
he was appointed Vice Consul at Málaga where he remained until 1930, when he
returned to the United States and accepted appointment as an assistant to an Assistant
Secretary in the Department of State, in which capacity he served until appointed
tohis present position.
THE WHITE HOUSE ,
February 10, 1933 .
HERBERT HOOVER
[No. 6022-A]

```

```text
Executive Order 6067. March 3, 1933
Executive Order
APPOINTMENT OF THOMAS H. ALLEN
Thomas H. Allen may be appointed special investigator in the Immigration
Service of the Department of Labor without regard to the requirements of the civil-
service rules . This employee has rendered highly meritorious service under most
trying and difficult circumstances , and I feel the issuance of this order is warranted .
HERBERT HOOVER
THE WHITE HOUSE ,
March 3, 1933 .
[No. 6067 ]
```

### Te7157343ba713bd1382c8020

Text hash e60589bc2f6f7ea67fc255f9895645f919ee755c6e76c46bfb24da90653d1fd3; absolute characters 0–1574; files: jobs/mabillon-held-printings/output/texts/EO_6773.txt

```text
DEPOSITED BY THE
UNITED "FATT OF CO MERICA

AUG 13 4

g (
AG

Executive Order

EXEMPTION OF CARLETON W. STURTEVANT FROM COMPULSORY
RETIREMENT FOR AGE

WHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404;
5 U.S.C., sec. 692b), provides:

“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch
or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the
District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for auto-
matic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in
such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary:
Provided, That the President may, by Executive order, exempt from the provisions
of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires

* * *

AND WHEREAS the public interest requires that Carleton W. Sturtevant,
principal engineer, Engineer Department at Large, War Department, who was 70
years of age on January 24, 1934, and who was exempted from compulsory retire-
ment for a period of 6 months by Executive Order No. 6577, dated January 25, 1934,
be further exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service
until November 1, 1935;

NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the authority vested in me by the aforesaid
section, I do hereby exempt Carleton W. Sturtevant from the provisions thereof
and continue him in the service until November 1, 1935.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT.

Tre Wuits Hovse,
June 80 1934.

[No. 6773]

U. 8. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1934

```

```text
Executive Order 6063. March 2, 1933
Executive Order
APPOINTMENT OF MISS ELLEN T. PURCELL AND MISS J. ELIZABETH WILLIAMS
Miss Ellen T. Purcell and Miss J. Elizabeth Williams may be appointed to
classified positions in the Department of Labor without regard to the requirements
of the civil -service rules .
These young ladies have for some time been employed in the Office of the Secre-
tary of Labor and have performed their duties in an excellent and efficient manner .
This order is issued upon the recommendation of the Secretary of Labor .
THE WHITE HOUSE ,
March 2, 1933 .
[No. 6063 ]

```

{"confidence":"medium","events":[{"actor_id":"EO:6998","char_end":3344,"char_start":1456,"confidence":"medium","how_named":"direct","id":"CHAIN:EO:6088#1","notes":"Change wording lead; chronology does not decide legal effect.","provision_text":"11. Any person violating any of the provisions of these regulations shall be\npunished by a fine of not more than $500 or imprisoned in jail for not more than 6\nmonths, or by both, and in addition the license of any such person may be revoked\nor suspended, as provided in the Act of June 19, 1934 (ch. 657, 48 Stat. 1116).\n\nFRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT\nTue Wuite Hovss,\nMarch 26, 1935.\n\n[No. 6997]\n\nU.S. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1938\n\n“MES!\n\nf\n\no~\n\nExecutibe @rder\n\nx\n\nEXEMPTION OF Harry O. BAILEY FROM COMPULSORY RETIREMENT FOR\nAGE\n\nWHEREAS section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 (ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404;\n5 U.S. C., sec. 692b), provides:\n\n“On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or\nservice of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District\nof Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic\nseparation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such\nservice, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Pro-\nvided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of\nthis section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires: * * *”;\n\nAND WHEREAS the public interest requires that Harry O. Bailey, Superin-\ntendent of Meters, Office of the Architect of the Capitol, who was exempted from\ncompulsory retirement for a period of 2 years by Executive Order No. 6088, dated\nMarch 29, 1933, be further exempted from the provisions of this section and continued\nin the service until April 1, 1937;\n\nNOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the authority vested in me by the aforesaid\nsection, I do hereby further exempt Harry O. Bailey from the provisions thereof and\ncontinue him in the service until April 1, 1937.\n\nFRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT\nTue WuHire Hovse,\nMarch 80, 1936.\n\n[No. 6998]\n\nU.S. GOVERNMENT PRINTING OFFICE: 1998","sections_touched":["section 204"],"signing_date":"1935-03-30","source":{"acceptable_as_original":false,"filing_date":null,"grade":"machine_read_government_printed_copy","instrument_id":"EO:6998","location_confidence":"high","location_verified":false,"page_or_item":"Executive_Orders_6936-7081.djvu; scanned pages 212-213; closing number tag 6998","requires_page_check":false,"sha256":"b8302c12c7566389dd8dec67a8f463e171c0038d0c86bbd8c0b950e21970d617","signing_date":"1935-03-30","text_path":"jobs/mabillon-held-printings/output/texts/EO_6998.txt","text_sha256":"7be170819eddadb71d20f7642fd45703fd4fdb7967553709c694f755585ada35","url":"https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Executive_Orders_6936-7081.djvu"},"verb":"revoke"}],"generic_refs_excluded":235,"id":"CHAIN:EO:6088","notes":"Named, located change provisions only; missing provisions and unheld texts remain dossier gaps. Roosevelt). Text and identity: read in full in the registered American Presidency Project transcription (jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts; grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; standing note: original not yet held). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl has a heading naming EO 6088, its date (March 29, 1933) appears in the held text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. The dossier's original and clause text cite the APP page harvested live (jobs/app-executive-orders/output/DOCUMENTS.jsonl, sha256 f6ccd51d...), whose custody cannot be re-proven once the site changes; a common-crawl mirror capture of the same page is also held and registered (sha256 abbf450a..., MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl row with WARC location) and its text file is byte-identical to the live one (text_sha256 6103fadf... for both). The quoted clause words are therefore re-provable from the mirror capture. The end cites the order's complete held text (ORIGINAL:EO:6088), not the dossier's own-terms record taken from the mirror file: the claim checker's custody registry (SOURCES.jsonl) holds no record of the mirror capture hash and resolves the page URL to the live bytes, so a citation of the mirror record fails as 'held_but_different_hash' even though its text is identical. Both page records were checked: each names EO 6088, its date March 29, 1933 appears in the text, and each text_sha256 equals the held file's. It exempts Harry O. Bailey, superintendent of meters in the District of Columbia under the Office of the Architect of the Capitol from section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 until March 31, 1935. Identity: number, date, person and end date agree across the APP page, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Legal reading: a single time-limited grant that ends by its own terms on 1935-03-31, inclusive. Later orders naming it, recorded as later events with their own dates and quotes: EO 6998 (March 30, 1935) recites that Bailey 'was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of 2 years by Executive Order No. 6088, dated March 29, 1933' and further exempts him until April 1, 1937; EO 7597 (March 31, 1937) recites EO 6088 and EO 6998 ('periods totaling four years') and further exempts Bailey until July 1, 1937. Each recites this order as past history and grants a new period; none revokes or amends this order, so none is this order's end. Searches made on 2026-10-28: (1) census titles in jobs/domesday-register/output/REGISTER.csv with retirement wording for the surname; (2) every WPA 1944 numbered-list entry (jobs/x1-class-engine/data/WPA_NUMBERED_ENTRIES.csv, whitespace-insensitive) for the surname with retirement wording; (3) a full-text search of every held order text under jobs/*/output/texts and jobs/*/data for the person's full name within reach of 'retire' or 'exempt'; (4) a citation search of every held order text for 'Order No. 6088' and list forms ('Orders Nos. ... 6088'). They found this order and the later orders recorded as later events, and nothing else naming this employee. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Held texts of later exemptions show the President still granting exemptions under section 204 through and after this date (EO 7725 of October 12, 1937, running to November 1, 1938, and EO 7842 of March 15, 1938, running to June 1, 1939), which corroborates that the statutory scheme ran through the stated date.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms end on the stated 'until' date. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The employee's death or separation before the date would leave the exemption unused but is not, on the order's text, an end of the order, and no held record shows such an event. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act. Later orders naming it, recorded as later events with their own dates and quotes: EO 6998 (March 30, 1935) recites that Bailey 'was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of 2 years by Executive Order No. 6088, dated March 29, 1933' and further exempts him until April 1, 1937; EO 7597 (March 31, 1937) recites EO 6088 and EO 6998 ('periods totaling four years') and further exempts Bailey until July 1, 1937. Each recites this order as past history and grants a new period; none revokes or amends this order, so none is this order's end.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of Harry O. Bailey from automatic separation. The recital of section 204, any recital of an earlier exemption and the identification of the employee are not independent commands.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and pay earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects. Any annuity or retirement-credit consequence flows from the retirement statutes, not from the order, and does not keep the order operative: the order commands nothing after its stated date. fbd2515753679f1002259760c5bec413380c514109a8040e746","current_canonical":false,"locations":[{"file_sha256":"ceaf6352de32096abab2a7d8480f6e2335b40c795380bab06a2467c30a8bc2f7","path":"research/excavator/claude-production-2026-10-28/b1b/packet/supp/applied-v4/CLAIMS.proposed.jsonl","pointer":"line:4"},{"file_sha256":"ceaf6352de32096abab2a7d8480f6e2335b40c795380bab06a2467c30a8bc2f7","path":"research/excavator/claude-production-2026-10-28/b1b/packet/supp/complete-v1/CLAIMS.selected.jsonl","pointer":"line:4"},{"file_sha256":"ceaf6352de32096abab2a7d8480f6e2335b40c795380bab06a2467c30a8bc2f7","path":"research/excavator/claude-production-2026-10-28/b1b/packet/supp/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-production-2026-10-28/b1b/packet/supp/applied-v4/CLAIMS.proposed.jsonl","pointer":"line:4"}],"reported_material_gaps":[],"research_rationale":"EO 6088 (March 29, 1933, Franklin D. Roosevelt). Text and identity: read in full in the registered American Presidency Project transcription (jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts; grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; standing note: original not yet held). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl has a heading naming EO 6088, its date (March 29, 1933) appears in the held text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. The dossier's original and clause text cite the APP page harvested live (jobs/app-executive-orders/output/DOCUMENTS.jsonl, sha256 f6ccd51d...), whose custody cannot be re-proven once the site changes; a common-crawl mirror capture of the same page is also held and registered (sha256 abbf450a..., MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl row with WARC location) and its text file is byte-identical to the live one (text_sha256 6103fadf... for both). The quoted clause words are therefore re-provable from the mirror capture, and the end cites the own-terms record taken from the registered mirror capture, whose custody copy is committed under jobs/cotton-capture-custody/output. Both page records were checked: each names EO 6088, its date March 29, 1933 appears in the text, and each text_sha256 equals the held file's. It exempts Harry O. Bailey, superintendent of meters in the District of Columbia under the Office of the Architect of the Capitol from section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 until March 31, 1935. Identity: number, date, person and end date agree across the APP page, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Legal reading: a single time-limited grant that ends by its own terms on 1935-03-31, inclusive. Later orders naming it, recorded as later events with their own dates and quotes: EO 6998 (March 30, 1935) recites that Bailey 'was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of 2 years by Executive Order No. 6088, dated March 29, 1933' and further exempts him until April 1, 1937; EO 7597 (March 31, 1937) recites EO 6088 and EO 6998 ('periods totaling four years') and further exempts Bailey until July 1, 1937. Each recites this order as past history and grants a new period; none revokes or amends this order, so none is this order's end. Searches made on 2026-10-28: (1) census titles in jobs/domesday-register/output/REGISTER.csv with retirement wording for the surname; (2) every WPA 1944 numbered-list entry (jobs/x1-class-engine/data/WPA_NUMBERED_ENTRIES.csv, whitespace-insensitive) for the surname with retirement wording; (3) a full-text search of every held order text under jobs/*/output/texts and jobs/*/data for the person's full name within reach of 'retire' or 'exempt'; (4) a citation search of every held order text for 'Order No. 6088' and list forms ('Orders Nos. ... 6088'). They found this order and the later orders recorded as later events, and nothing else naming this employee. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Held texts of later exemptions show the President still granting exemptions under section 204 through and after this date (EO 7725 of October 12, 1937, running to November 1, 1938, and EO 7842 of March 15, 1938, running to June 1, 1939), which corroborates that the statutory scheme ran through the stated date.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms end on the stated 'until' date. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The employee's death or separation before the date would leave the exemption unused but is not, on the order's text, an end of the order, and no held record shows such an event. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act. Later orders naming it, recorded as later events with their own dates and quotes: EO 6998 (March 30, 1935) recites that Bailey 'was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of 2 years by Executive Order No. 6088, dated March 29, 1933' and further exempts him until April 1, 1937; EO 7597 (March 31, 1937) recites EO 6088 and EO 6998 ('periods totaling four years') and further exempts Bailey until July 1, 1937. Each recites this order as past history and grants a new period; none revokes or amends this order, so none is this order's end.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of Harry O. Bailey from automatic separation. The recital of section 204, any recital of an earlier exemption and the identification of the employee are not independent commands.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and pay earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects. Any annuity or retirement-credit consequence flows from the retirement statutes, not from the order, and does not keep the order operative: the order commands nothing after its stated date. The later further-exemption orders recorded as later events rest on their own grant under section 204; they recite this order as past history and do not continue it.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause, one named person; no partial amendment or revocation of this order is held or found. The later further-exemption orders are separate grants for the same employee, recorded as later Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl (an archived capture whose archive and WARC location are recorded) has a heading naming this order number, a date and subject agreeing with the held text, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the clause-drafts copy. EO 6577 (January 25, 1934), read in full, exempts Carleton W. Sturtevant, principal engineer, Engineer Department at Large, War Department, from section 204 of the Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until July 31, 1934. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page record, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Later orders naming the same employee are held and added to this packet as source-supplement excerpts: EO 6773 (June 30, 1934) recites that Sturtevant 'was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of 6 months by Executive Order No. 6577' and further exempts him until November 1, 1935; EO 7217 (October 30, 1935) recites Executive Orders No. 6577 and No. 6773 ('a period of twenty-one months') and further exempts him until May 1, 1936; this text is a live-harvested APP page (DOCUMENTS.jsonl only, no archived capture), so its custody cannot be re-proven once the site changes. Each is recorded as a later event on the clause (clauses.csv). Extension reading, decided on the texts: each later order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own period. None says that this order 'is amended' or alters its date; each recites the earlier exemption as a past grant ('who was exempted ... by Executive Order No. ...') and grants a further exemption 'until' a new date. The strike-and-insert ruling does not apply because nothing is struck or inserted. So this order's own command ended on its own stated date, inclusive (exclusive alternative: ended when that day began), and each later order is recorded as a later event naming the same employee; the employee's exempt status continued without a gap under the later orders. The alternative reading, that the later orders prolonged this order's command, would put its first end at 1936-05-01 (inclusive); the texts do not support it, because no later order operates on this order's text. Legal reading: a time-limited grant that ends by its own terms on 1934-07-31, inclusive (exclusive alternative: it stopped when that day began). The census, WPA and full-text name searches found no further order naming this employee beyond those listed. Searches made on 2026-10-28: census register titles (jobs/domesday-register/output/REGISTER.csv) and the WPA 1944 numbered list (jobs/x1-class-engine/data/WPA_NUMBERED_ENTRIES.csv, whitespace-insensitive) for the employee's surname among compulsory-retirement orders, and a full-text search of every held order text under jobs/*/output/texts for the employee's name. The machine USC and eCFR citation searches (complete, 0 hits) exclude a current-law reference; the OFR catalog search starts in 1937 and is not relied on alone. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Held texts of later exemptions show the President still granting exemptions under section 204 after this date (for example EO 7704 of September 10, 1937, running to October 1, 1938, and EO 7725 of October 12, 1937, running to November 1, 1938), which corroborates that the statutory scheme ran through the stated date.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms end on the stated 'until' date. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act. No held record shows the employee's death or separation before that date; if such a record is found, the claim must be reopened to decide whether the exemption lapsed earlier. No held order amends, revokes or shortens this order. Extension reading, decided on the texts: each later order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own period. None says that this order 'is amended' or alters its date; each recites the earlier exemption as a past grant ('who was exempted ... by Executive Order No. ...') and grants a further exemption 'until' a new date. The strike-and-insert ruling does not apply because nothing is struck or inserted. So this order's own command ended on its own stated date, inclusive (exclusive alternative: ended when that day began), and each later order is recorded as a later event naming the same employee; the employee's exempt status continued without a gap under the later orders. Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl (an archived capture whose archive and WARC location are recorded) has a heading naming this order number, a date and subject agreeing with the held text, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the clause-drafts copy. EO 6214 (July 25, 1933), read in full, exempts Curtis F. Marbut, Principal Soil Scientist, Department of Agriculture, from section 204 of the Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until June 30, 1934. The order states that he \"during the current month, will reach the retirement age\"; it is in the short form (\"is hereby exempted from the provisions of that section until June 30, 1934\") without the usual whereas recital. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page record, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Later orders naming the same employee are held and added to this packet as source-supplement excerpts: EO 6738 (June 14, 1934) recites that Marbut 'was exempted until June 30, 1934, from compulsory retirement by Executive Order No. 6214' and further exempts him until July 1, 1935; EO 7080 (June 19, 1935) recites Executive Orders Nos. 6214 and 6738 and further exempts him until July 1, 1936; this text is a live-harvested APP page (DOCUMENTS.jsonl only, no archived capture), so its custody cannot be re-proven once the site changes. Each is recorded as a later event on the clause (clauses.csv). Extension reading, decided on the texts: each later order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own period. None says that this order 'is amended' or alters its date; each recites the earlier exemption as a past grant ('who was exempted ... by Executive Order No. ...') and grants a further exemption 'until' a new date. The strike-and-insert ruling does not apply because nothing is struck or inserted. So this order's own command ended on its own stated date, inclusive (exclusive alternative: ended when that day began), and each later order is recorded as a later event naming the same employee; the employee's exempt status continued without a gap under the later orders. The alternative reading, that the later orders prolonged this order's command, would put its first end at 1936-07-01 (inclusive); the texts do not support it, because no later order operates on this order's text. Legal reading: a time-limited grant that ends by its own terms on 1934-06-30, inclusive (exclusive alternative: it stopped when that day began). The census, WPA and full-text name searches found no further order naming this employee beyond those listed. Searches made on 2026-10-28: census register titles (jobs/domesday-register/output/REGISTER.csv) and the WPA 1944 numbered list (jobs/x1-class-engine/data/WPA_NUMBERED_ENTRIES.csv, whitespace-insensitive) for the employee's surname among compulsory-retirement orders, and a full-text search of every held order text under jobs/*/output/texts for the employee's name. The machine USC and eCFR citation searches (complete, 0 hits) exclude a current-law reference; the OFR catalog search starts in 1937 and is not relied on alone. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Held texts of later exemptions show the President still granting exemptions under section 204 after this date (for example EO 7704 of September 10, 1937, running to October 1, 1938, and EO 7725 of October 12, 1937, running to November 1, 1938), which corroborates that the statutory scheme ran through the stated date.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms end on the stated 'until' date. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act. No held record shows the employee's death or separation before that date; if such a record is found, the claim must be reopened to decide whether the exemption lapsed earlier. No held order amends, revokes or shortens this order. Extension reading, decided on the texts: each later order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own period. None says that this order 'is amended' or alters its date; each recites the earlier exemption as a past grant ('who was exempted ... by Executive Order No. ...') and grants a further exemption 'until' a new date. The strike-and-insert ruling does not apply because nothing is struck or inserted. So this order's own command ended on its own stated date, inclusive (exclusive alternative: ended when that day began), and each later order is recorded as a later event naming the same employee; the employee's exempt status continued without a gap under the later orders. The alternative reading, that the later orders prolonged this order's command, would put its first end at the last later order's 'until' date (1936-07-01, inclusive); the texts do not support it, because no later order operates on this order's text.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of one named employee. The recital of section 204 and the identification of the employee are not independent commands.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and pay earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects. Any annuity or retirement-credit consequence flows from the retirement statutes, not from the order, and does not keep the order operative: the order commands nothing after its stated date. Later orders (EO 6738 and EO 7080) recite it as a past grant and grant new exemptions; they do not keep this order operative.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause, one named person; the later further-exemption orders are recorded as later events and do not amend this order.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"598b801998b90c1a6da1a9c693191b2e33b12e1c069394fc9dc601a6642eca67","path":"research/excavator/claude-production-2026-10-28/b1a/packet/supp/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-production-2026-10-28/b1a/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:17","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"598b801998b90c1a6da1a9c693191b2e33b12e1c069394fc9dc601a6642eca67","path":"research/excavator/claude-production-2026-10-28/b1a/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:17","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:6214#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"e66f7c3f694ac20de8aba81e64c78e4232f5e4929b9a06f7234c80e4ba2a5bf1","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6214-exemption-curtis-f-marbut-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"e66f7c3f694ac20de8aba81e64c78e4232f5e4929b9a06f7234c80e4ba2a5bf1","state":"match"},"sha256":"ac3247d428881757b4ab295f9f545c1278826845b428b6f99db1a2b06f42b29c","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6214-exemption-curtis-f-marbut-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"e66f7c3f694ac20de8aba81e64c78e4232f5e4929b9a06f7234c80e4ba2a5bf1"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"82f51f9663389870994004c847e6027eeedb7550e7fa12eea5383c37ab46851c","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6738-exemption-curtis-f-marbut-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"82f51f9663389870994004c847e6027eeedb7550e7fa12eea5383c37ab46851c","state":"match"},"sha256":"4cd8088adeab19962c97dcc7723e3362258123ee47ac252afbf95e35ec4a4cd9","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/out grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; standing note: original not yet held). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl (an archived capture whose archive and WARC location are recorded) has a heading naming this order number, a date and subject agreeing with the held text, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the clause-drafts copy. EO 6338 (October 14, 1933), read in full, exempts two employees, James S. Goldsmith, Superintendent of Buildings and Labor of the United States National Museum, and George G. Hedgcock, senior pathologist, Department of Agriculture, from section 204 of the Act of June 30, 1932 and continues them in the service until October 31, 1934. Identity: number, date, persons and titles agree across the APP page record, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list (which spells the second name 'Hedgecock'). Later orders naming the same employees are held and added to this packet as source-supplement excerpts: EO 6862 (September 30, 1934) recites that Hedgcock 'was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of 1 year by Executive Order No. 6338' and further exempts him until November 1, 1935; EO 6873 (October 16, 1934) recites that Goldsmith 'was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of 1 year by Executive Order No. 6338' and further exempts him until November 1, 1935; EO 7208 (October 19, 1935) recites Executive Orders No. 6338 and No. 6862 and further exempts Hedgcock until November 1, 1936; this text is a live-harvested APP page (DOCUMENTS.jsonl only, no archived capture), so its custody cannot be re-proven once the site changes; EO 7475 (October 19, 1936) recites Executive Orders No. 6338, 6862 and 7208 and further exempts Hedgcock until July 1, 1937; EO 7645 (June 24, 1937) recites Executive Orders No. 6338, 6862, 7208 and 7475 ('periods totaling three years and eight months') and further exempts Hedgcock until January 1, 1938. Each is recorded as a later event on the clause (clauses.csv). Extension reading, decided on the texts: each later order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own period. None says that this order 'is amended' or alters its date; each recites the earlier exemption as a past grant ('who was exempted ... by Executive Order No. ...') and grants a further exemption 'until' a new date. The strike-and-insert ruling does not apply because nothing is struck or inserted. So this order's own command ended on its own stated date, inclusive (exclusive alternative: ended when that day began), and each later order is recorded as a later event naming the same employee; the employee's exempt status continued without a gap under the later orders. The alternative reading, that the later orders prolonged this order's command, would put its first end at 1935-11-01 for Goldsmith (EO 6873) and 1938-01-01 for Hedgcock (EO 7645), inclusive; the texts do not support it, because no later order operates on this order's text. The dossier's actor context EO 7645 and its note 'target provision not located: EO:7645' concern that further-exemption order, which recites this order but changes nothing in it; its text is held and recorded as a later event. Legal reading: one time-limited grant to two named employees that ends by its own terms on 1934-10-31, inclusive, for both (exclusive alternative: it stopped when that day began). The census, WPA and full-text name searches found no further order naming either employee beyond those listed. Searches made on 2026-10-28: census register titles (jobs/domesday-register/output/REGISTER.csv) and the WPA 1944 numbered list (jobs/x1-class-engine/data/WPA_NUMBERED_ENTRIES.csv, whitespace-insensitive) for the employee's surname among compulsory-retirement orders, and a full-text search of every held order text under jobs/*/output/texts for the employee's name. The machine USC and eCFR citation searches (complete, 0 hits) exclude a current-law reference; the OFR catalog search starts in 1937 and is not relied on alone. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Held texts of later exemptions show the President still granting exemptions under section 204 after this date (for example EO 7704 of September 10, 1937, running to October 1, 1938, and EO 7725 of October 12, 1937, running to November 1, 1938), which corroborates that the statutory scheme ran through the stated date.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms end on the stated 'until' date. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act. No held record shows the employee's death or separation before that date; if such a record is found, the claim must be reopened to decide whether the exemption lapsed earlier. No held order amends, revokes or shortens this order. Extension reading, decided on the texts: each later order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own period. None says that this order 'is amended' or alters its date; each recites the earlier exemption as a past grant ('who was exempted ... by Executive Order No. ...') and grants a further exemption 'until' a new date. The strike-and-insert ruling does not apply because nothing is struck or inserted. So this order's own command ended on its own stated date, inclusive (exclusive alternative: ended when that day began), and each later order is recorded as a later event naming the same employee; the employee's exempt status continued without a gap under the later orders. The alternative reading would put the first end at 1935-11-01 for Goldsmith and 1938-01-01 for Hedgcock (inclusive); the texts do not support it, because no later order operates on this order's text.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of two named employees with one stated date. The recital of section 204 and the identification of the employees are not independent commands.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and pay earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects. Any annuity or retirement-credit consequence flows from the retirement statutes, not from the order, and does not keep the order operative: the order commands nothing after its stated date. 6467 (November 27, 1933, Franklin D. Roosevelt) is read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6467-code-fair-competition-for-the-retail-trade-order-no-7-extension.txt; grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; APP node 362290; wayback capture 2023-09-05T15:52:28Z). APP page check under AGENT_PROTOCOL.md: the MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl row with the same sha256 (36a085929dd7...) has heading 'Executive Order 6467—Code of Fair Competition for the Retail Trade, Order No. 7, Extension of Effective Date of Article IX, Section 4' and date 1933-11-27, the heading's number and subject and the date agree with the text (which ends 'The White House, ...' with the same date), and the row's text_sha256 equals the held file's text_sha256 (d3000ab7eec6...). The text is complete (operative paragraph, signature, recommendation line and date). Identity: the census register (jobs/domesday-register/output/REGISTER.csv) title 'Executive Order 6467—Code of Fair Competition for the Retail Trade, Order No. 7, Extension of Effective Date of Article IX, Section 4' dated 1933-11-27 and the WPA 1944 numbered-list summary 'Effectivedate of Article IX, Section 4, of Code of Fair Competition for the Retail Trade extended to July 1, 1934. III NRA Codes 660.' agree with the text. original not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved. Operative command: the order postpones the effective date of Article IX, section 4 of the Retail Trade code (the code approved October 21, 1933 by EO 6351): 'it is hereby ordered that the effective date of article IX, section 4, be extended to July 1, 1934, this order to become effective within 10 days of the date hereof unless cause to the contrary shall have been shown to the Administrator before that date.' Clause inventory: the machine inventory is accepted as complete (one clause, the operative paragraph with its signature and recommendation lines). First end: by its own terms on 1934-07-01, the date to which the order extends the effective date of Article IX, section 4; the later repeal (S.J. Res. 113, June 14, 1935) is a later event after the postponement had run. Effect on later law: none; the postponement had expired about eleven months before the repeal.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own terms control. The order fixes July 1, 1934 as the date to which the effective date of Article IX, section 4 is extended; on that date the provision takes effect and the postponement has nothing left to postpone. The dossier's fixed-term own-terms record (TERM:record) states 1934-07-01. The end is recorded as 1934-07-01, the stated date; the postponement covered the days before that date (last postponed day June 30, 1934), and the alternative inclusive reading of the stated date would carry it through the end of July 1, 1934. The order's own 10-day proviso ('unless cause to the contrary shall have been shown to the Administrator') is a condition on its start; no held record shows cause was shown. The held texts name no later order that further extends the effective date of Article IX, section 4. The original approval of the Retail Trade code is EO 6351 (October 21, 1933). Searches on 2026-10-29 of held evidence: the held order texts (jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts: every order numbered 6180 through 7076 that mentions codes of fair competition, NRA or the National Industrial Recovery Act was scanned for the words cancel, revoke, rescind, supersede, terminate, annul and void; every held text folder except the Federal Register and source-ladder folders was searched for citations of this order's number and for the code's name); the census-register titles (jobs/domesday-register/output/REGISTER.csv); and the 1933-1935 summaries of the WPA 1944 numbered list (jobs/x1-class-engine/data/WPA_NUMBERED_ENTRIES.csv). The only held cancellation of a code approval in the period is EO 6925-A (December 19, 1934), which cancels the approval of the Cinders, Ashes and Scavenger Trade code after a hearing, under Article VIII of that code and Title I. No held record cancels, revokes, suspends or supersedes this code before June 14, 1935. General orders that touch approved codes are not ends of this clause: EO 6354 (October 23, 1933) as amended by EO 6710 (May 15, 1934) excepts small local retail and service employers in towns under 2,500 population from code provisions on hours, pay, minimum prices and assessments; EO 6723 (May 26, 1934) suspends code provisions other than child labor, maximum hours, minimum pay and sections 7(a) and 10(b) for service trades the Administrator later designates. Each reaches only a class of persons or of designated trades and leaves the approval operative for everyone else; no held record designates this code's trade under EO 6723. The repeal on June 14, 1935 and the Schechter decision of May 27, 1935 both fall after July 1, 1934 and are later events for this clause; Schechter is a validity characterization, not an end.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order's only operative command is the postponement; it appoints no one, delegates nothing and directs no agency. The recital that the postponement allows 'manufacturers of coupons and other forms of scrip the opportunity to adjust their business' states a purpose, not a command.\",\"savings_check\":\"Acts and omissions while Article IX, section 4 was postponed are accrued; the postponement commanded nothing after July 1, 1934. From July 1, 1934 the provision operated as part of the Retail Trade code under its own terms, and its later fate belongs to the code approval (EO 6351), not to this order. The order's 10-day effectiveness proviso and the repeal of June 14, 1935 do not continue the postponement.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause: the postponement of the effective date of Article IX, section 4. It does not approve, amend or exempt anything else in the code. EO 6354 as amended by EO 6710 and EO 6723 do not reach Article IX, section 4 (coupons and scrip): EO 6710 concerns hours, pay, minimum prices and assessments.
