bdb7bb5df5d3782b1dc39bee2fb86bd034c18351f2c5a","state":"match"},"sha256":"36a085929dd7db7db03d9ce12114f403630967ca8929415bf46389d980b72069","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6467-code-fair-competition-for-the-retail-trade-order-no-7-extension.txt","text_sha256":"d3000ab7eec60171320bdb7bb5df5d3782b1dc39bee2fb86bd034c18351f2c5a"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"d3000ab7eec60171320bdb7bb5df5d3782b1dc39bee2fb86bd034c18351f2c5a","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6467-code-fair-competition-for-the-retail-trade-order-no-7-extension.txt","sha256":"d3000ab7eec60171320bdb7bb5df5d3782b1dc39bee2fb86bd034c18351f2c5a","state":"match"},"sha256":"36a085929dd7db7db03d9ce12114f403630967ca8929415bf46389d980b72069","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6467-code-fair-competition-for-the-retail-trade-order-no-7-extension.txt","text_sha256":"d3000ab7eec60171320bdb7bb5df5d3782b1dc39bee2fb86bd034c18351f2c5a"}]},{"claim_sha256":"ec8dc337f4b39ac122b716dcd23be6750953e9e11c1c3cd5553c218f4842f0ad","current_canonical":false,"locations":[{"file_sha256":"1537e4690b133e7ea9ea39fd5d8771ffb913144aa37af67cb1d0839a6c222c0e","path":"research/excavator/claude-nira-sweep-2026-10-29/t1/packet/supp/applied-v1/CLAIMS.proposed.jsonl","pointer":"line:10"},{"file_sha256":"acf596facb92128b977454b8504f61d32c505882eacac07abe1c8573542a33ce","path":"research/excavator/claude-nira-sweep-2026-10-29/t1/packet/supp/applied-v2/CLAIMS.proposed.jsonl","pointer":"line:9"}],"reported_material_gaps":[],"research_rationale":"EO 6467 (November 27, 1933, Franklin D. Roosevelt) is read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6467-code-fair-competition-for-the-retail-trade-order-no-7-extension.txt; grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; APP node 362290; wayback capture 2023-09-05T15:52:28Z). APP page check under AGENT_PROTOCOL.md: the MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl row with the same sha256 (36a085929dd7...) has heading 'Executive Order 6467—Code of Fair Competition for the Retail Trade, Order No. 7, Extension of Effective Date of Article IX, Section 4' and date 1933-11-27, the heading's number and subject and the date agree with the text (which ends 'The White House, ...' with the same date), and the row's text_sha256 equals the held file's text_sha256 (d3000ab7eec6...). The text is complete (operative paragraph, signature, recommendation line and date). Identity: the census register (jobs/domesday-register/output/REGISTER.csv) title 'Executive Order 6467—Code of Fair Competition for the Retail Trade, Order No. 7, Extension of Effective Date of Article IX, Section 4' dated 1933-11-27 and the WPA 1944 numbered-list summary 'Effectivedate of Article IX, Section 4, of Code of Fair Competition for the Retail Trade extended to July 1, 1934. III NRA Codes 660.' agree with the text. original not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved. Operative command: the order postpones the effective date of Article IX, section 4 of the Retail Trade code (the code approved October 21, 1933 by EO 6351): 'it is hereby ordered that the effective date of article IX, section 4, be extended to July 1, 1934, this order to become effective within 10 days of the date hereof unless cause to the contrary shall have been shown to the Administrator before that date.' Clause inventory: the machine inventory is accepted as complete (one clause, the operative paragraph with its signature and recommendation lines). First end: by its own terms on 1934-07-01, the date to which the order extends the effective date of Article IX, section 4; the later repeal (S.J. Res. 113, June 14, 1935) is a later event after the postponement had run. Effect on later law: none; the postponement had expired about eleven months before the repeal.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own terms control. The order fixes July 1, 1934 as the date to which the effective date of Article IX, section 4 is extended; on that date the provision takes effect and the postponement has nothing left to postpone. The dossier's fixed-term own-terms record (TERM:record) states 1934-07-01. The end is recorded as 1934-07-01, the stated date; the postponement covered the days before that date (last postponed day June 30, 1934), and the alternative inclusive reading of the stated date would carry it through the end of July 1, 1934. The order's own 10-day proviso ('unless cause to the contrary shall have been shown to the Administrator') is a condition on its start; no held record shows cause was shown. The held texts name no later order that further extends the effective date of Article IX, section 4. The original approval of the Retail Trade code is EO 6351 (October 21, 1933). Searches on 2026-10-29 of held evidence: the held order texts (jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts: every order numbered 6180 through 7076 that mentions codes of fair competition, NRA or the National Industrial Recovery Act was scanned for the words cancel, revoke, rescind, supersede, terminate, annul and void; every held text folder except the Federal Register and source-ladder folders was searched for citations of this order's number and for the code's name); the census-register titles (jobs/domesday-register/output/REGISTER.csv); and the 1933-1935 summaries of the WPA 1944 numbered list (jobs/x1-class-engine/data/WPA_NUMBERED_ENTRIES.csv). The only held cancellation of a code approval in the period is EO 6925-A (December 19, 1934), which cancels the approval of the Cinders, Ashes and Scavenger Trade code after a hearing, under Article VIII of that code and Title I. No held record cancels, revokes, suspends or supersedes this code before June 14, 1935. General orders that touch approved codes are not ends of this clause: EO 6354 (October 23, 1933) as amended by EO 6710 (May 15, 1934) excepts small local retail and service employers in towns under 2,500 population from code provisions on hours, pay, minimum prices and assessments; EO 6723 (May 26, 1934) suspends code provisions other than child labor, maximum hours, minimum pay and sections 7(a) and 10(b) for service trades the Administrator later designates. Each reaches only a class of persons or of designated trades and leaves the approval operative for everyone else; no held record designates this code's trade under EO 6723. The repeal on June 14, 1935 and the Schechter decision of May 27, 1935 both fall after July 1, 1934 and are later events for this clause; Schechter is a validity characterization, not an end.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order's only operative command is the postponement; it appoints no one, delegates nothing and directs no agency. The recital that the postponement allows 'manufacturers of coupons and other forms of scrip the opportunity to adjust their business' states a purpose, not a command.\",\"savings_check\":\"Acts and omissions while Article IX, section 4 was postponed are accrued; the postponement commanded nothing after July 1, 1934. From July 1, 1934 the provision operated as part of the Retail Trade code under its own terms, and its later fate belongs to the code approval (EO 6351), not to this order. The order's 10-day effectiveness proviso and the repeal of June 14, 1935 do not continue the postponement.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause: the postponement of the effective date of Article IX, section 4. It does not approve, amend or exempt anything else in the code. EO 6354 as amended by EO 6710 and EO 6723 do not reach Article IX, section 4 (coupons and scrip): EO 6710 concerns hours, pay, minimum prices and assessments. The sole command is the postponement; it does not approve or amend the code.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"7da7e2cd1b78d5d90e27c74d1d3fa60d5e64613ab36c495a0e3a64f414befd88","path":"research/excavator/claude-nira-sweep-2026-10-29/t1/packet/supp/complete-v2/receipts/research/excavator/claude-nira-sweep-2026-10-29/t1/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.exposed-controls.jsonl","pointer":"line:11","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"7da7e2cd1b78d5d90e27c74d1d3fa60d5e64613ab36c495a0e3a64f414befd88","path":"research/excavator/claude-nira-sweep-2026-10-29/t1/packet/supp/complete-v3/receipts/research/excavator/claude-nira-sweep-2026-10-29/t1/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.exposed-controls.jsonl","pointer":"line:11","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"7da7e2cd1b78d5d90e27c74d1d3fa60d5e64613ab36c495a0e3a64f414befd88","path":"research/excavator/claude-nira-sweep-2026-10-29/t1/packet/supp/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.exposed-controls.jsonl","pointer":"line:11","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:6467#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"d3000ab7eec60171320bdb7bb5df5d3782b1dc39bee2fb86bd034c18351f2c5a","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6467-code-fair-competition-for-the-retail-trade-order-no-7-extension.txt","sha256":"d3000ab7eec60171320bdb7bb5df5d3782b1dc39bee2fb86bd034c18351f2c5a","state":"match"},"sha256":"36a085929dd7db7db03d9ce12114f403630967ca8929415bf46389d980b72069","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6467-code-fair-competition-for-the-retail-trade-order-no-7-extension.txt","text_sha256":"d3000ab7eec60171320bdb7bb5df5d3782b1dc39bee2fb86bd034c18351f2
